\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big(\frac{W}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big)^{m}\right](1+o(n^{-\beta}))

\displaystyle=p_{m}(1+o(n^{-\beta})),

where
\beta
and
\varepsilon
are sufficiently small and where we recall
p_{m}
from (
2.4
).

It remains to show that the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.29
) can be included in the
o(n^{-\beta})
term. The main difficulty here for the actual proof is controlling the partial sums
S_{\ell}
, which, for ‘too small’
\ell
, do not concentrate around the expected value with probability sufficiently close to one. In this simplified non-rigorous proof, however, the assumption that
S_{\ell}=(\ell+1)\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]
allows us to ignore this technical difficulty for now and focus on the main probabilistic arguments. The aim is to show that

(5.51)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{1\leq j<n^{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j)=m\right)\right]=o(p_{m}n^{-\beta}),

for some small
\beta
when
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small.

We first focus on the case that
m=\widetilde{c}\log n+o(\log n)
with
\widetilde{c}<1/\log\theta
. In this case, we can simply bound the conditional probability from above by one to obtain the upper bound
n^{-(1-\varepsilon)}
. Then, by Lemma
5.5
, we have that
p_{m}\geq(\theta+\xi)^{-m}=n^{-\widetilde{c}\log(\theta+\xi)}
for any
\xi>0
. Since
\widetilde{c}<1/\log\theta
, it follows that we can choose
\xi,\beta
, and
\varepsilon
sufficiently small such that
n^{-(1-\varepsilon)}=o(n^{-\beta-\widetilde{c}\log(\theta+\xi)})=o(p_{m}n^{-\beta})
, from which the claim in (
5.51
) follows.

When
\widetilde{c}\in[1/\log\theta,c)
instead (where we recall that
c<\theta/(\theta-1)
), a more careful approach is required.
In this case, we bound
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j)=m\right)\leq\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j)\geq m\right)
and use a Chernoff bound on the right-hand side of the inequality. This bound follows from the proof of
[
15
, Proposition
7.2
]
and yields

(5.52)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{1\leq j<n^{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j)=m\right)\right]\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{1\leq j<n^{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp(m(1-u_{j}+\log u_{j}))\right],

where

(5.53)

u_{j}:=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{\ell=j}^{n-1}\frac{W_{j}}{S_{\ell}}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{n}\frac{W_{j}}{\ell\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\leq\frac{\log(n/j)}{m\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\leq\frac{\log n}{m\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}=\frac{1+o(1)}{\widetilde{c}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}.

Note that the second step follows from our simplifying assumption on
S_{\ell}
and we bound
W_{j}
from above by one in the inequality. Using that
x\mapsto 1-x+\log x
is increasing on
(0,1)
, that
1/(\widetilde{c}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right])\leq\log\theta/(\theta-1)<1
, and that we have a bound for
u_{j}
uniformly in
1\leq j\leq n^{\varepsilon}
, (
5.52
) yields

(5.54)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{1\leq j<n^{\varepsilon}}\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j)=m\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{1-\varepsilon}}\exp\Big((1+o(1))\widetilde{c}\log n\Big(1-\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}+\log\Big(\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}\Big)\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=n^{-(1-\varepsilon)+(1+o(1))\widetilde{c}(1-1/(\widetilde{c}(\theta-1))+\log(1/(\widetilde{c}(\theta-1))))}.

The aim is to show that this last expression is
o(p_{m}n^{-\beta})
for some sufficiently small
\beta>0
. Again using that
p_{m}n^{-\beta}\geq n^{-\widetilde{c}\log(\theta+\xi)-\beta}
, it suffices to show that

(5.55)

-(1-\varepsilon)+\widetilde{c}\Big(1-\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}+\log\Big(\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}\Big)\Big)<-\widetilde{c}\log(\theta+\xi)-\beta

holds when we choose
\xi,\varepsilon,
and
\beta
sufficiently small. Since the right-hand side is continuous in
\xi
and
\beta
, and the left-hand side is continuous in
\varepsilon
, it suffices to prove that

(5.56)

-1+\widetilde{c}\Big(1-\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}+\log\Big(\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}\Big)\Big)<-\widetilde{c}\log\theta

holds for any
\theta\in(1,2]
and any
\widetilde{c}<\theta/(\theta-1)
. As the second derivative of the mapping
x\mapsto-1+x-1/(\theta-1)+x\log(1/(x(\theta-1)))
is negative and the mapping
x\mapsto-x\log\theta
is tangent to the former mapping at
x=\theta/(\theta-1)
, the inequality follows for all
\widetilde{c}<\theta/(\theta-1)
. This completes the proof of (
5.51
) for some sufficiently small
\varepsilon
and
\beta
. Combined with (
5.50
), this yields (
5.1
) for
k=1
.

5.3.
Complete proof of Proposition
5.1

Before we provide the complete proof, we first discuss the proof strategy. Though this is similar to the approach of the non-rigorous proof provided in the previous sub-section, dealing with
k>1
many uniformly selected vertices provides technical challenges worth addressing. Let us start by introducing the following notation: for two sequences
f(n)
and
g(n)
, we let
f(n)\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq g(n)
denote the set

(5.57)

\{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}:j_{\ell}\in[f(n),g(n)]\text{ for all }\ell\in[k],\text{ and }j_{\ell_{1}}\neq j_{\ell_{2}}\text{ for any }1\leq\ell_{1}<\ell_{2}\leq k\}.

In words, summing over all indices
f(n)\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq g(n)
denotes summing over all distinct indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
which are at least
f(n)
and at most
g(n)
.

The left-hand side of (
5.1
) can be expressed by conditioning on the values of the typical vertices, and splitting between cases of young and old vertices. That is,

(5.58)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(v_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon)}\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right),

where

(5.59)

I_{n}(\varepsilon):=\{\textbf{j}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}):1\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n,\ \exists i\in[k]\ j_{i}<n^{\varepsilon}\}

for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Splitting the sum on the first line into the two sums on the second and third line allows us to deal with them in a different way, and is similar to the distinction made in (
5.29
). In the sum on the second line, in which all indices are at least
n^{\varepsilon}
, we can apply the law of large numbers on sums of vertex-weights to gain more control over the conditional probability of the event
\{\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\}
. The aim is to show that this first sum has the desired form, as on the right-hand side of (
5.1
). This uses the same ideas as in the non-rigorous proof in the previous sub-section, but increases in difficulty due to multiple summations, integrals, and careful ‘book-keeping’ of a large number of distinct indices.

The sum on the third line, in which at least one of the indices takes on values strictly smaller than
n^{\varepsilon}
can be shown to be negligible compared to the first sum. Especially when
m_{\ell}
is large, this is non-trivial. To do this, we consider the tail events
\{\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\}
and use the negative quadrant dependence of the degrees (see Remark
5.2
and
[
15
, Lemma
7.1
]
), so that we can deal with the more tractable probabilities
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)
for all
\ell\in[k]
, rather than the probability of the intersection of all tail degree events. We observe that this is not required when
k=1
(that is, using the negative quadrant dependence), and significantly increases the technical difficulty of this part of the proof compared to the non-rigorous proof provided in the previous sub-section. Depending on whether the indices in
I_{n}(\varepsilon)
are at most or at least
n^{\varepsilon}
, we then use bounds similar to one developed in the proof of
[
15
, Lemma
7.1
]
or use an approach similar to what we use to bound the sum on the second line of (
5.58
), respectively.

In the following lemma, we deal with the sum on the second line of (
5.58
).

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
W
be a positive random variable that satisfies condition

(

C

1
)

of Assumption
2.3
. Consider the WRT model in Definition
2.1
with vertex-weights
(W_{i})_{i\in[n]}
which are i.i.d. copies of
W
and fix
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
c\in(0,\theta/(\theta-1))
. Then, there exist
\beta>0
and an
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
that can be made arbitrarily small, such that uniformly over non-negative integers
m_{\ell}<c\log n,\ell\in[k]
,

(5.60)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)=\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big(\frac{W}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big)^{m_{\ell}}\bigg]\big(1+o\big(n^{-\beta}\big)\big).

We note that condition

(

C

2
)

of Assumption
2.3
is not required for this result to hold. To prove this lemma, we sum over all possible
m_{\ell}
vertices that connect to
j_{\ell}
for each
\ell\in[k]
and use the fact that the
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
are at least
n^{\varepsilon}
to precisely control the connection probabilities and to evaluate the sums over all the possible
m_{\ell}
vertices for all
\ell\in[k]
, as well as the sum over the indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
.

In the following lemma, we show the sum on the third line of (
5.58
) is negligible compared to the sum on the second line.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
W
be a positive random variable that satisfies conditions

(

C

1
)

and

(

C

2
)

of Assumption
2.3
. Consider the WRT model in Definition
2.1
with vertex-weights
(W_{i})_{i\in[n]}
which are i.i.d. copies of
W
. Fix
k\in\mathbb{N},c\in(0,\theta/(\theta-1))
and recall
I_{n}(\varepsilon)
from (
5.59
). There exist
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and a
\beta>0
such that uniformly over non-negative integers
m_{\ell}<c\log n,\ell\in[k]
,

(5.61)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon)}\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)=o\bigg(\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big(\frac{W}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big)^{m_{\ell}}\right]n^{-\beta}\bigg).

Note that condition

(

C

2
)

of Assumption
2.3
is required only in this lemma, where it was not necessary in Lemma
5.10
. In fact, it is required for one inequality in the proof only, which convinces us that it could possibly be avoided.

It is clear that (
5.1
) in Proposition
5.1
immediately follows from using the results of Lemmas
5.10
and
5.11
in (
5.58
). Namely, in Lemma
5.10
we can take
\varepsilon
arbitrarily small and the left-hand side of the equality in Lemma
5.11
is monotone decreasing as
\varepsilon
decreases, so that we can take
\varepsilon
as small as required. In what follows we first prove Lemma
5.10
in Section
5.4
, prove Lemma
5.11
in Section
5.5
, and finally complete the proof of Proposition
5.1
in Section
5.6
. After each of the proofs we discuss the required adaptations to prove the results of Lemmas
5.10
and
5.11
for the model with
random out-degree
, as introduced in Remark
2.2
(ii)
.

5.4.
Proof of Lemma
5.10

Proof of Lemma
5.10
.

We provide a matching upper bound and lower bound for

(5.62)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right).

Upper bound.

Let
\zeta_{n}=n^{-\delta\varepsilon}/\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]
for some
\delta\in(0,1/2)
. We define the event

(5.63)

E^{(1)}_{n}:=\bigg\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{j}W_{\ell}\in((1-\zeta_{n})\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]j,(1+\zeta_{n})\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]j),\text{ for all }n^{\varepsilon}\leq j\leq n\bigg\},

We use
E^{(1)}_{n}
to mimic the simplified assumption that
S_{j}=(j+1)\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]
in the non-rigorous proof in Section
5.2
. We know that
\mathbb{P}((E^{(1)}_{n})^{c})=o(n^{-\gamma})
for any
\gamma>0
(and thus of smaller order than
\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{m_{\ell}}n^{-\beta}
for any
\beta>0
and uniformly in
m_{1},\ldots m_{k}<(\theta/(\theta-1))\log n
) from Lemma
A.2
in the
Appendix
. By also conditioning on the vertex-weights, this yields for any
\gamma>0
the upper bound

(5.64)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}{}

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}^{(1)}}]+o(n^{-\gamma}),

Now, to express the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.64
), we consider ordered indices rather than unordered ones. We provide details for the case
n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}<j_{2}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n
and discuss later on how the other permutations of
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
can be dealt with. Moreover, for every
\ell\in[k]
, we introduce the ordered indices
j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\ell\in[k]
, which denote the steps at which vertex
\ell
increases it degree by one. Note that for every
\ell\in[k]
these indices are distinct by definition, but we also require that
i_{s,\ell}\neq i_{t,j}
for any
\ell,j\in[k],s\in[m_{\ell}]
and
t\in[m_{j}]
(equality is allowed only when
\ell=j
and
s=t
). Indeed, a new vertex can only connect to one already present vertex. We denote this constraint by adding a
*
on the summation symbol. Finally, we define
j_{k+1}:=n
. Combining these additional steps, we arrive at

(5.65)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}{}

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(1)}_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\ \,\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{W_{j_{t}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_{s,t}-1}W_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{u=1}^{k}\!\!\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=j_{u}+1\\
s\neq i_{\ell,t},\ell\in[m_{t}],t\in[k]\end{subarray}}^{j_{u+1}}\!\!\!\!\bigg(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{j_{\ell}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{s-1}W_{\ell}}\bigg)\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(1)}_{n}}\Bigg].

This step is equivalent to (
5.31
) in the non-rigorous proof. The terms in the first double product denote the probabilities that vertices
i_{s,t}
connect to
j_{t}
, whereas the terms in the second double product denote the probabilities that vertices
i_{s,t}
do
not
connect to the vertices
j_{\ell}
such that
j_{\ell}<i_{s,t}
. Similar to (
5.32
) in the non-rigorous proof, we then include the terms where
s=i_{\ell,t}
for all
\ell\in[m_{t}]
and
t\in[k]
in the second double product. To do this, we change the first double product to

(5.66)

\prod_{t=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{W_{j_{t}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_{s,t}-1}W_{\ell}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}W_{j_{\ell}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{i_{s,t}>j_{\ell}\}}}\leq\prod_{t=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{W_{j_{t}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_{s,t}-1}W_{\ell}-k},

that is, we subtract the vertex-weight
W_{j_{\ell}}
in the numerator when the vertex
j_{\ell}
has already been introduced by step
i_{s,t}
. In the upper bound we use that the weights are bounded from above by one. We thus arrive at the upper bound

(5.67)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\ \,\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{W_{j_{t}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_{s,t}-1}W_{\ell}-k}\prod_{u=1}^{k}\prod_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\bigg(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{j_{\ell}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{s-1}W_{\ell}}\bigg)\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(1)}_{n}}\Bigg].

For ease of writing, we omit the first sum until we actually intend to sum over the indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
. We use the bounds from the event
E^{(1)}_{n}
to bound

(5.68)

\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_{s,t}-1}W_{\ell}\geq(i_{s,t}-1)\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n}),\qquad\sum_{\ell=1}^{s-1}W_{\ell}\leq s\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}).

For
n
sufficiently large, we observe that
(i_{s,t}-1)\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n})-k\geq i_{s,t}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-2\zeta_{n})
, which yields

(5.69)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{W_{j_{t}}}{i_{s,t}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-2\zeta_{n})}\prod_{u=1}^{k}\prod_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\!\!\bigg(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{j_{\ell}}}{s\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})}\bigg)\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(1)}_{n}}\Bigg].

Moreover, relabelling the vertex-weights
W_{j_{t}}
to
W_{t}
for
t\in[k]
does not change the distribution of the terms within the expected value, so that the expected value remains unchanged. We can also bound the indicator from above by one, to arrive at the upper bound

(5.70)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{W_{t}}{i_{s,t}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-2\zeta_{n})}\prod_{u=1}^{k}\prod_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\bigg(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}}{s\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})}\bigg)\Bigg].

We bound the final product from above by

(5.71)

\displaystyle\prod_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\bigg(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}}{s\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})}\sum_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}}{s}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}\log\Big(\frac{j_{u+1}}{j_{u}+1}\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{j_{u+1}}{j_{u}+1}\Big)^{-\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}))}.

As the weights are almost surely bounded by one, we thus find

(5.72)

\prod_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\bigg(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}}{s\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})}\bigg)\leq\Big(\frac{j_{u+1}}{j_{u}}\Big)^{-\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}))}\Big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\Big),

which is equivalent to (
5.36
) in the non-rigorous proof.
As a result, we obtain the upper bound

(5.73)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\,\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}{}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{k}\!\bigg(\Big(\frac{W_{t}}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\Big)^{m_{t}}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{1}{i_{s,t}(1-2\zeta_{n})}\bigg)\!\prod_{u=1}^{k}\!\Big(\frac{j_{u+1}}{j_{u}}\Big)^{-\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}))}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\times\Big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\Big).

As
j_{k+1}=n
, we can simplify the final product to obtain

(5.74)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\,\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!(1-2\zeta_{n})^{-\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[{}

\displaystyle\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{W_{t}}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\Big)^{m_{t}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(j_{t}^{W_{t}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}))}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}i_{s,t}^{-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\times n^{-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}W_{\ell}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}))}\Bigg]\Big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\Big).

We bound this from above even further by no longer constraining the indices
i_{s,t}
to be distinct. That is, for distinct
t_{1}
and
t_{2}\in[k]
, we allow
i_{s_{1},t_{1}}=i_{s_{2},t_{2}}
to hold for any
s_{1}\in[m_{t_{1}}]
and
s_{2}\in[m_{t_{2}}]
. This yields

(5.75)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!(1-2\zeta_{n})^{-\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[{}

\displaystyle\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{W_{t}}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\Big)^{m_{t}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(j_{t}^{W_{t}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}))}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}i_{s,t}^{-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\times n^{-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}W_{\ell}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}))}\Bigg]\Big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\Big).

We set

(5.76)

a_{t}:=W_{t}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})),

and look at the terms

(5.77)

\frac{n^{-\sum_{t=1}^{k}a_{t}}}{(n)_{k}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\bigg((a_{t}(1+\zeta_{n}))^{m_{t}}j_{t}^{a_{t}}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}i_{s,t}^{-1}\bigg).

We bound the sums from above by multiple integrals, almost surely, which yields

(5.78)

\displaystyle\frac{n^{-\sum_{t=1}^{k}a_{t}}}{(n)_{k}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}(a_{t}(1+\zeta_{n}))^{m_{t}}j_{t}^{a_{t}}\int_{j_{t}}^{n}\int_{x_{1,t}}^{n}\cdots\int_{x_{m_{t}-1,t}}^{n}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}x_{s,t}^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}x_{m_{t},t}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{1,t}.

Using (
A.21
) in Lemma
A.4
, we obtain that this equals

(5.79)

\frac{n^{-\sum_{t=1}^{k}a_{t}}}{(n)_{k}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}(a_{t}(1+\zeta_{n}))^{m_{t}}j_{t}^{a_{t}}\frac{(\log(n/j_{t}))^{m_{t}}}{m_{t}!}.

Substituting this in (
5.77
) and reintroducing the sum over the indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
, we arrive at

(5.80)

\frac{(1+\zeta_{n})^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{j_{t}}{n}\Big)^{a_{t}}\frac{(a_{t}\log(n/j_{t}))^{m_{t}}}{m_{t}!}.

We observe that switching the order of the indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
achieves the same result as permuting the
m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}
and
a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}
. Hence, if we let
\pi:[k]\to[k]
be a permutation, then considering the indices
n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\pi(1)}<j_{\pi(2)}<\ldots<j_{\pi(k)}\leq n
yields a similar result as in (
5.80
) but with a term
j_{\pi(t)}^{a_{\pi(t)}}(\log(n/j_{\pi(t)}))^{m_{\pi(t)}}/m_{\pi(t)}!
in the final product. Since this product is invariant to such permutations of the
m_{t}
and
a_{t}
, the only thing that would change is the summation order of the indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
.

By using (
5.80
) in (
5.75
) and incorporating all permutations of the indices, i.e.
n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\pi(1)}<j_{\pi(2)}<\ldots<j_{\pi(k)}\leq n
for all
\pi\in P_{k}
(where
P_{k}
denotes the set of all permutations on
[k]
), we arrive at

(5.81)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}{}

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell},\ell\in[k]\right)\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(1)}_{n}}]

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\Big(\frac{1+\zeta_{n}}{1-2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\pi\in P_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\pi(1)}<\ldots<j_{\pi(k)}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{j_{\pi(t)}}{n}\Big)^{a_{t}}\frac{(a_{t}\log(n/j_{\pi(t)}))^{m_{t}}}{m_{t}!}\right]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\Big(\frac{1+\zeta_{n}}{1-2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{j_{t}}{n}\Big)^{a_{t}}\frac{(a_{t}\log(n/j_{t}))^{m_{t}}}{m_{t}!}\right].

We bound the last expression from above even further by allowing the indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
to take
any
integer value in
[n^{\varepsilon},n]
. That is,
j_{\ell_{1}}=j_{\ell_{2}}
is allowed for
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
. As this introduces more non-negative terms, it yields the upper bound

(5.82)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\Big(\frac{1+\zeta_{n}}{1-2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\leq n}\cdots{}

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{k}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{j_{t}}{n}\Big)^{a_{t}}\frac{(a_{t}\log(n/j_{t}))^{m_{t}}}{m_{t}!}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\Big(\frac{1+\zeta_{n}}{1-2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}{}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j\leq n}\Big(\frac{j}{n}\Big)^{a_{t}}\frac{(a_{t}\log(n/j))^{m_{t}}}{m_{t}!}\right],

where we note that the product can be taken out in the second line, since the weights are independent. We now observe two things: First, we can redefine
a_{t}=a:=W/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}))
. After all, the index of the vertex-weights
W_{1},\ldots,W_{k}
is irrelevant, since they are independent and identically distributed. Second, the summand is equal to
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(P(a)=m_{t}\right)
, where
P(a)\sim\text{Poi}(a\log(n/j))
, conditionally on
W
. So we obtain, similar to (
5.40
) in the non-rigorous proof,

(5.83)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\Big(\frac{1+\zeta_{n}}{1-2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j\leq n}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(P(a)=m_{t}\right)\right].

We now use the duality between Poisson and gamma random variables, as also explained in the intuitive proof after (
5.40
). That is, let
G_{t}\sim\text{Gamma}(m_{t},1)
and
V\sim\text{Exp}(1)
be independent random variables. Then,
\{P(a)=m_{t}\}=\{G_{t}\leq a\log(n/j),G_{t}+V>a\log(n/j)\}
, so that

(5.84)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(P(a)=m_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(G_{t}\leq a\log(n/j),G_{t}+V>a\log(n/j)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}\leq\log(n/j),Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}>\log(n/j)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}\leq\log(n/j)\right)-\mathbb{P}_{W}(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\leq\log(n/j)),

where
Y_{t}\sim\text{Gamma}(m_{t},a)
and
\widetilde{V}\sim\text{Exp}(a)
, conditionally on
W
(and observe that
Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\sim\text{Gamma}(m_{t}+1,a)
). We thus obtain

(5.85)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\Big(\frac{1+\zeta_{n}}{1-2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j\leq n}\big(\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}\leq\log(n/j)\right)-\mathbb{P}_{W}(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\leq\log(n/j))\big)\right],

similar to (
5.45
) in the non-rigorous proof. We now compare the sum of the gamma probabilities with an integral. That is, since both probabilities are decreasing in
j
, we can bound the sum from above by

(5.86)

\displaystyle\int_{\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor}^{n}{}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}\leq\log(n/x)\right)\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{W}(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\leq\log(n/x))\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=n\int_{0}^{\log(n/\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor)}\mathrm{e}^{-y}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}\leq y\right)\,\mathrm{d}y-n\int_{0}^{\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)}\mathrm{e}^{-y}\mathbb{P}_{W}(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\leq y)\,\mathrm{d}y,

where we use a variable substitution
y=\log(n/x)
to obtain the second line. Writing the cumulative density function as an integral from zero to
y
and switching the order of integration, we find

(5.87)

\displaystyle n{}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{W}[\mathrm{e}^{-Y_{t}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{t}\leq\log(n/\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor)\}}]-n\mathbb{E}_{W}[\mathrm{e}^{-(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\leq\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\}}]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{W}(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\leq\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil))+\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor\big(\mathbb{P}_{W}(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\leq\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil))-\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}\leq\log(n/\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor)\right)\big).

As the last term is negative, we can omit it to obtain an upper bound. The first term on the second line can be bounded from above by

(5.88)

\mathbb{P}_{W}(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\leq\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil))\leq\mathbb{P}_{W}(\mathrm{e}^{-(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V})}\geq n^{-(1-\varepsilon)})\leq n^{1-\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{-(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V})}\right]=n^{1-\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}+1}.

Combined, this yields the upper bound

(5.89)

n\mathbb{E}_{W}[\mathrm{e}^{-Y_{t}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{t}\leq\log(n/\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor)\}}]-n\mathbb{E}_{W}[\mathrm{e}^{-(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\leq\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\}}]+n^{1-\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}+1}.

We now distinguish two cases:

(1)

m_{t}=c_{t}\log n+o(\log n)
with
c_{t}\in[0,1/(\theta-1)]
, for all
t\in[k]
.

(2)

m_{t}=c_{t}\log n+o(\log n)
with
c_{t}\in(1/(\theta-1),c)
, for some
t\in[k]
.

In case

(
1
)

, we bound the difference of the expected values from above by

(5.90)

\displaystyle n{}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{W}[\mathrm{e}^{-Y_{t}}]-n\mathbb{E}_{W}[\mathrm{e}^{-(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V})}]+n\mathbb{E}_{W}[\mathrm{e}^{-(Y_{t}+\widetilde{V})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}>\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\}}]+n^{1-\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}+1}

\displaystyle\leq n\frac{1}{1+a}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-\varepsilon}))+\mathcal{O}(n^{\varepsilon}).

Substituting this for the argument of the expected value for each
t\in[k]
in (
5.85
) yields

(5.91)

\Big(\frac{1+\zeta_{n}}{1-2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-\varepsilon}))\prod_{t=1}^{k}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{1+a}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(n^{-(1-\varepsilon)})\bigg).

We recall that
a:=W/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}))
and that
\zeta_{n}:=n^{-\delta\varepsilon}/\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]
. Since
W\in[0,1]
almost surely and
m_{t}=\mathcal{O}(\log n)
,

(5.92)

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{1+a}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big(\frac{W}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big)^{m_{t}}\right](1+o(n^{-\beta}))=p_{m_{t}}(1+o(n^{-\beta})),

for some small
\beta>0
. We have by Lemma
5.5
that
p_{m_{t}}\geq(\theta+\xi)^{-m_{t}}
for any
\xi>0
. Now we use that in case

(
1
)

m_{t}=c_{t}\log n+o(\log n)
with
c_{t}<1/(\theta-1)
. As a result, for
\xi
,
\varepsilon,
and some
\beta>0
sufficiently small and since
\log\theta<\theta-1
for
\theta>1
, we then have
n^{-(1-\varepsilon)}=o(n^{-(c_{t}+o(1))\log(\theta+\xi)-\beta})=o(p_{m_{t}}n^{-\beta})
. Together with (
5.92
), this implies we can write the argument of the product in (
5.91
) as
p_{m_{t}}(1+o(n^{-\beta}))
whenever
m_{t}
satisfies case

(
1
)

.

In case

(
2
)

, these bounds do not suffice. First of all, we note that because of the product structure in (
5.85
), we can deal with any
terms that satisfy
c_{t}\in[0,1/(\theta-1)]
in the same way as in

(
1
)

. Without loss of generality we thus assume that
c_{t}\in(1/(\theta-1),c)
for all
t\in[k]
. In this case, we use that for any
N>0
,

(5.93)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{W}[\mathrm{e}^{-Y_{t}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{t}\leq N\}}]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{N}\frac{a^{m_{t}}}{\Gamma(m_{t})}x^{m_{t}-1}\mathrm{e}^{-(1+a)x}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\int_{0}^{N}\frac{(1+a)^{m_{t}}}{\Gamma(m_{t})}x^{m_{t}-1}\mathrm{e}^{-(1+a)x}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}^{\prime}\leq N\right),

where
Y_{t}^{\prime}\sim\text{Gamma}(m_{t},1+a)
, conditionally on
W
. As
Y_{t}+\widetilde{V}\sim\text{Gamma}(m_{t}+1,a)
, we also obtain a similar result for the second expected value in (
5.89
) with a random variable
Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}\sim\text{Gamma}(m_{t}+1,1+a)
. As in the non-rigorous proof, we can assume that
Y_{t}^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}
are defined on the same probability space and we have that
Y_{t}^{\prime}\preceq Y_{t}^{\prime\prime}
. Using this in (
5.89
), we thus obtain

(5.94)

\displaystyle n{}

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}^{\prime}\leq\log(n/\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor)\right)-\frac{a}{1+a}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}^{\prime\prime}\leq\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\right)\bigg]+n^{1-\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}+1}

\displaystyle\leq n\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\bigg[\frac{1}{1+a}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y^{\prime}_{t}\leq\log(n/\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor)\right)+\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y^{\prime}_{t}\leq\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil),Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}>\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\leq Y^{\prime}_{t}\leq\log(n/\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}\rfloor)\right)\bigg]+n^{1-\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}+1}.

By omitting the probability from the first term and bounding the other two probabilities from above by
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}>\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\right)
, we obtain an upper bound

(5.95)

n\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\Big(\frac{1}{1+a}(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-\varepsilon}))+2\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}>\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\right)\Big).

Again, we substitute this for the argument in the expected value in (
5.85
) to obtain for some large constant
C>0
,

(5.96)

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1+\zeta_{n}}{1-2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-\varepsilon}))\prod_{t=1}^{k}\bigg({}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(\frac{1}{1+a}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\right]

\displaystyle+{}

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\big(\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}>\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\right)+n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\right]\bigg).

It thus remains to show that the second expected value in (
5.96
) is of sufficiently smaller order compared to the first. We obtain this by using the indicators
\mathbbm{1}_{\{(1+a)<c_{t}/(1-\mu)\}}
and
\mathbbm{1}_{\{1+a\geq c_{t}/(1-\mu)\}}
to split the expected value in two parts, for some
\mu>0
small enough such that
c_{t}/(1-\mu)<\theta/(\theta-1)
(which is possible since
c_{t}<c<\theta/(\theta-1)
). For the first indicator, we can bound

(5.97)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\big(\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}>\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\right)+n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1+a<c_{t}/(1-\mu)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{c_{t}/(1-\mu)-1}{c_{t}/(1-\mu)}\Big)^{m_{t}}

\displaystyle=\Big(1-\frac{1-\mu}{c_{t}}\Big)^{m_{t}}<(\theta+\xi)^{-m_{t}},

for some small
\xi>0
and
n
sufficiently large. Since the first expected value in (
5.96
) equals
p_{m_{t}}\geq(\theta+\xi/2)^{-m_{t}}
, where the inequality follows from Lemma
5.5
, we thus obtain that

(5.98)

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}>\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1+a<c_{t}/(1-\mu)\}}\bigg]=o\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\frac{1}{1+a}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\bigg]n^{-\beta}\bigg),

for some small
\beta>0
, since
m_{t}=c_{t}\log n+o(\log n)
with
c_{t}\in[1/\log\theta,c)
. For the second indicator, we observe that
\{Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}>\log(n/\lceil n^{\varepsilon}\rceil)\}=\{Z_{t}>(1+a)(1-\varepsilon)\log n(1+o(1))\}
, where
Z_{t}\sim\text{Gamma}(m_{t}+1,1)
. We then have

(5.99)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}{}

\displaystyle\bigg[\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Z_{t}>(1+a)(1-\varepsilon)\log n(1+o(1))\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\{1+a\geq c_{t}/(1-\mu)\}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\bigg]\mathbb{P}\!\left(Z_{t}>\frac{c_{t}(1-\varepsilon)}{1-\mu}\log n(1+o(1))\right).

It thus suffices to show that the probability on the right-hand side is
o(n^{-\beta})
for some small
\beta>0
. By choosing
\varepsilon\in(0,\mu)
we can apply a standard large deviation bound. Let
(V_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be i.i.d. exponential rate
1
random variables and let
I(a):=a-1-\log(a)
be their rate function. Then, as we can think of
Z_{t}
as the sum of
V_{1},\ldots,V_{m_{t}+1}
,

(5.100)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(Z_{t}\geq\frac{c_{t}(1-\varepsilon)}{1-\mu}\log n(1+o(1))\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\!\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m_{t}+1}V_{i}\geq(m_{t}+1)\frac{c_{t}(1-\varepsilon)\log n(1+o(1))}{(1-\mu)(m_{t}+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(-(m_{t}+1)I\Big(\frac{c_{t}(1-\varepsilon)\log n(1+o(1))}{(1-\mu)(m_{t}+1)}\Big)\Big).

In the first step, we express the upper bound within the probability in terms of the mean of the sum of random variables, which equals
m_{t}+1
. We then use the large deviations bound in the second step, which we can do as the argument of
I
is strictly greater than
1
when
n
is sufficiently large (as
m_{t}+1\sim c_{t}\log n
) and
\varepsilon\in(0,\mu)
is sufficiently small. Since
I((c_{t}+o(1))(1-\varepsilon)\log n/((1-\mu)(m_{t}+1)))=(1-\varepsilon)/(1-\mu)-1-\log((1-\varepsilon)/(1-\mu))+o(1)
, we thus arrive at

(5.101)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(Z_{t}\geq\frac{c_{t}(1-\varepsilon)}{1-\mu}\log n(1+o(1))\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{-c_{t}\log n((1-\varepsilon)/(1-\mu)-1-\log((1-\varepsilon)/(1-\mu))+o(1))}

\displaystyle=n^{-c_{t,\mu,\varepsilon}+o(1)},

where
c_{t,\mu,\varepsilon}:=c_{t}((1-\varepsilon)/(1-\mu)-1-\log((1-\varepsilon)/(1-\mu)))>0
as
\varepsilon<\mu
and
x-1-\log x>0
holds for
x>1
. This yields (
5.98
) when using
\mathbbm{1}_{\{1+a\geq c_{t}/(1-\mu)\}}
for
\mu
and
\varepsilon
sufficiently small as well. Together with (
5.92
), this yields the desired result.

Combined with the analysis in case

(
1
)

, and the fact that

(5.102)

\Big(\frac{1+\zeta_{n}}{1-2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}(1+\mathcal{O}(n^{-\varepsilon}))=1+o(n^{-\beta}),

for some small
\beta>0
, by the choice of
\zeta_{n}
, and the fact that
m_{t}=\mathcal{O}(\log n)
for all
t\in[k]
, it follows that (
5.91
) is at most
(o(n^{-\beta})\prod_{t=1}^{k}p_{m_{t}})
for some small
\beta>0
in both cases

(
1
)

and

(
2
)

. Finally, using this in (
5.64
) yields

(5.103)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}{}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)\leq\prod_{t=1}^{k}\big(p_{m_{t}}(1+o(n^{-\beta}))\big)+o(n^{-\gamma}).

As we can choose
\gamma
arbitrarily large (as discussed prior to (
5.64
)), this term can incorporated in the
o(n^{-\beta})
term regardless of the choice of
m_{t}
, which completes the proof of the upper bound.

Lower bound.

We then focus on proving a similar lower bound. We define the event

(5.104)

E^{(2)}_{n}:=\Big\{\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{j}W_{\ell}\in(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n})j,\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})j),\text{ for all }n^{\varepsilon}\leq j\leq n\Big\}.

We again have from Lemma
A.2
in the
Appendix
that
\mathbb{P}((E^{(2)}_{n})^{c})=o(n^{-\gamma})
for any
\gamma>0
. We obtain a lower bound for the probability of the event
\{\mathcal{Z}_{n}(v_{\ell})=m_{\ell},\ell\in[k]\}
by omitting the second term in (
5.64
). This yields, together with the rearrangements in (
5.65
),

(5.105)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}{}

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}<j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\!\prod_{t=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{W_{j_{t}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_{s,t}-1}W_{\ell}}\prod_{u=1}^{k}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=j_{u}+1\\
s\neq i_{\ell,t},\ell\in[m_{t}],t\in[k]\end{subarray}}^{j_{u+1}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\bigg(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{j_{\ell}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{s-1}W_{\ell}}\bigg)\Bigg].

We again start by only considering the ordered indices
n^{\varepsilon}<j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}
and also omit this sum for now for ease of writing. We also omit the constraint
s\neq i_{\ell,t},\ell\in[m_{t}],t\in[k]
in the final product. As this introduces more terms smaller than one, we obtain a lower bound. Then, in the two denominators, we bound the vertex-weights
W_{j_{1}},\ldots,W_{j_{k}}
from above and below by one and zero, respectively, to obtain a lower bound

(5.106)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{W_{j_{t}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_{s,t}-1}W_{\ell}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\ell\neq j_{t},t\in[k]\}}+k}\prod_{u=1}^{k}\prod_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\!\!\!\bigg(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{j_{\ell}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{s-1}W_{\ell}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\ell\neq j_{t},t\in[k]\}}}\bigg)\Bigg].

As a result, we can now swap the labels of
W_{j_{t}}
and
W_{t}
for each
t\in[k]
, which again does not change the expected value, but it changes the value of the two denominators to
\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{i_{s,t}}W_{\ell}+k
and
\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{i_{s,t}}W_{\ell}
, respectively. After this we introduce the indicator
\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(2)}_{n}}
and use the bounds in
E^{(2)}_{n}
on these sums in the expected value to obtain a lower bound. Finally, we note that the (relabelled) weights
W_{t},t\in[k],
are independent of
E^{(2)}_{n}
so that we can take the indicator out of the expected value. Combining all of the above steps, we arrive at the lower bound

(5.107)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}{}

\displaystyle\sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{W_{t}}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\Big)^{m_{t}}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{1}{i_{s,t}(1+2\zeta_{n})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{u=1}^{k}\prod_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\bigg(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}}{(s-1)\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n})}\bigg)\Bigg]\mathbb{P}(E^{(2)}_{n}).

The
1+2\zeta_{n}
in the fraction on the first line arises from the fact that, for
n
sufficiently large,
(i_{s,t}-1)(1+\zeta_{n})+k\leq i_{s,t}(1+2\zeta_{n})
. As stated above,
\mathbb{P}(E^{(2)}_{n})=1-o(n^{-\gamma})
for any
\gamma>0
. Similar to the calculations in (
5.71
) and using
\log(1-x)\geq-x-x^{2}
for
x
small, we obtain an almost sure lower bound for the final product for
n
sufficiently large of the form

(5.108)

\displaystyle\prod_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\bigg(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}}{(s-1)\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}\sum_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\frac{1}{s-1}

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n})}\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}\Big)^{2}\!\sum_{s=j_{u}+1}^{j_{u+1}}\!\frac{1}{(s-1)^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\Big(\frac{j_{u+1}}{j_{u}}\Big)^{-\sum_{\ell=1}^{u}W_{\ell}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n}))}\Big(1-\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\Big).

Using this in (
5.107
) yields the lower bound

(5.109)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!(1+2\zeta_{n})^{-\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{W_{t}}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\Big)^{m_{t}}\Big(\frac{j_{t}}{n}\Big)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}i_{s,t}^{-1}\Big)\Bigg]\Big(1-\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\Big),

where
\widetilde{a}_{t}:=W_{t}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n}))
. As in the upper bound, we can take the product over
t\in[k]
out of the expected value by the independence of the vertex-weights. This yields

(5.110)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\Big(\frac{1-\zeta_{n}}{1+2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\widetilde{a}_{t}^{m_{t}}\Big(\frac{j_{t}}{n}\Big)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}i_{s,t}^{-1}\Bigg]\Big(1-\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\Big).

As
\zeta_{n}=n^{-\delta\varepsilon}/\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]
and
m_{h}=\mathcal{O}(\log n)
for all
h\in[k]
, the fraction and the
1-\mathcal{O}(n^{-\varepsilon})
can be combined to yield a
1-o(n^{-\beta})
, for some small
\beta>0
. We now reintroduce the sum over the indices
n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n
and bound the sum over the indices
i_{s,\ell}
from below. We note that the expression in the expected value is decreasing in
i_{s,\ell}
and we restrict the range of the indices to
j_{\ell}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\ell\in[k]
, but no longer constrain the indices to be distinct (so that we can drop the
*
in the sum). In the distinct sums and the suggested lower bound, the number of values the
i_{s,\ell}
take on equal

(5.111)

\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\binom{n-(j_{\ell}-1)-\sum_{h=1}^{\ell-1}m_{t}}{m_{h}}\quad\text{and}\quad\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\binom{n-(j_{\ell}-1)-\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}{m_{\ell}},

respectively. It is straightforward to see that the former allows for more possibilities than the latter, as
\binom{b}{c}>\binom{a}{c}
when
b>a\geq c
. As we omit the largest values of the expected value (since it decreases in
i_{s,\ell}
and we omit the largest values of
i_{s,\ell}
), we thus arrive at the lower bound

(5.112)

\displaystyle((n)_{k})^{-1}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}<j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n-\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\!\prod_{t=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\widetilde{a}_{t}^{m_{t}}\Big(\frac{j_{t}}{n}\Big)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}\!\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{1}{i_{s,t}}\bigg]\big(1-o(n^{-\beta})\big),

where we also restrict the range of indices in the upper bound of the outer sum, as otherwise there would be a contribution of zero from these values of
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
. We now use similar techniques compared to the upper bound of the proof to switch from summation to integration. First, we observe that we can switch the inner summation and the product over
t
. Second, we restrict the upper bound on the outer sum to
n-1-2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}
. This will prove useful later. Combined, this yields

(5.113)

\displaystyle((n)_{k})^{-1}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}<j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n-1-2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\bigg[\widetilde{a}_{t}^{m_{t}}\Big(\frac{j_{t}}{n}\Big)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}\!\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{1}{i_{s,t}}\bigg](1-o(n^{-\beta})).

Then, we take the inner summation in the expected value and, for now, focus on

(5.114)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{t}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}<i_{1,t}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}i_{s,t}^{-1}\geq\int_{j_{t}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}+1}^{n+1}\int_{x_{1,t}+1}^{n+1}\cdots\int_{x_{m_{t}-1,t}+1}^{n+1}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}x_{s,t}^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}x_{m_{t},t}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{1,t}.

Since
j_{\ell}\leq n-1-2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}
for each
\ell\in[k]
, it follows in particular that
j_{\ell}+1+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\leq n
. It thus follows that we can use (
A.22
) in Lemma
A.4
with
a=j_{\ell}+1+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h},b=n+1
, and
k=m_{\ell}
to obtain the lower bound

(5.115)

\prod_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{m_{t}!}\log\Big(\frac{n+1}{j_{t}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}+m_{t}}\Big)^{m_{t}}\geq\prod_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{m_{t}!}\Big(\log\Big(\frac{n}{j_{t}+2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\Big)\Big)^{m_{t}}.

Substituting this in (
5.113
) yields the lower bound

(5.116)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}<j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n-1-2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big(\frac{j_{t}}{n}\Big)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}\frac{\widetilde{a}_{t}^{m_{t}}}{m_{t}!}\Big(\log\Big(\frac{n}{j_{t}+2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\Big)\Big)^{m_{t}}\bigg]\big(1-o(n^{-\beta})\big).

Note that, due to the independence of the vertex-weights
W_{1},\ldots,W_{k}
, and hence of
\widetilde{a}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{a}_{k}
, we can interchange the product and the sum with the expected value. To simplify the summation over
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
, we write the summand as

(5.117)

\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(j_{t}+2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\Big)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}\frac{n^{-\widetilde{a}_{t}}}{m_{t}!}\Big(\log\Big(\frac{n}{j_{t}+2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\Big)\Big)^{m_{t}}\bigg(1-\frac{2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}{j_{t}+2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\bigg)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}.

Using that
m_{t}<c\log n,j_{t}\geq n^{\varepsilon}
and almost surely,
x^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}\geq x^{1/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n}))}
for
x\in[0,1]
, we obtain the lower bound

(5.118)

\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(j_{t}+2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\Big)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}\frac{n^{-\widetilde{a}_{t}}}{m_{t}!}\Big(\log\Big(\frac{n}{j_{t}+2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}}\Big)\Big)^{m_{t}}\big(1-o(n^{-\beta})\big).

We can then shift the bounds on the range of the sum to
n^{\varepsilon}+2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}
and
n-1
. As
j_{k}=n
yields a contribution of zero to the sum, we can increase the upper range on the summation from
n-1
to
n
. We thus obtain the lower bound

(5.119)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}<j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{j_{t}}{n}\Big)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}\frac{1}{m_{t}!}(\log(n/j_{t}))^{m_{t}}\right]\big(1-o(n^{-\beta})\big).

We can now use a similar approach as in (
5.81
) through (
5.89
). By considering all permutations of the indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
, we obtain

(5.120)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}{}

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{t})=m_{t}\text{ for all }t\in[k]\right)]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-o(n^{-\beta})}{(n)_{k}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+2\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}<j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{k}(j_{t}/n)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}\frac{1}{m_{t}!}(\widetilde{a}_{t}\log(n/j_{t}))^{m_{t}}\Bigg].

We now aim to again exchange the order of summation and the product in the expected value at the expense of some additional terms. To this end, we allow the indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
to take on the same values, but also subtract the largest terms from the sum to still obtain a lower bound. We are thus required to determine which terms are the largest. The following holds for any
t\in[k]
:
j_{t}\mapsto(j_{t}/n)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}(\widetilde{a}_{t}\log(n/j_{t}))^{m_{t}}/m_{t}!
is increasing on
[1,n\exp(-m_{t}/\widetilde{a}_{t}))
and decreasing on
(n\exp(-m_{t}/\widetilde{a}_{t}),n]
, with maximum value
\mathrm{e}^{-m_{t}}m_{t}^{m_{t}}/m_{t}!
. As such, we again distinguish the two cases

(
1
)

and

(
2
)

as in the upper bound (i.e.
m_{t}=c_{t}\log n+o(\log n)
with either
c_{t}\leq 1/(\theta-1)
for all
t\in[k]
or
c_{t}>1/(\theta-1)
for some
t\in[k]
). In case

(
2
)

, we know that
n\exp(-m_{t}/\widetilde{a}_{t})\leq n^{1-c_{t}(\theta-1)(1+o(1))}=o(1)
, so that
(j_{t}/n)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}(\widetilde{a}_{t}\log(n/j_{t}))^{m_{t}}/m_{t}!
is maximised for the smallest value of
j_{t}
. Omitting these terms thus yields a lower bound. In case

(
1
)

, this is not the case, and hence we omit the largest terms at
n\exp(-m_{t}/\widetilde{a}_{t})
.

Let us deal with case

(
1
)

first. Since the sums contain

(5.121)

\Big(n-\Big\lceil n^{\varepsilon}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\Big\rceil+1\Big)_{k}

many terms, we bound these sums from below by

(5.122)

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\leq j_{1}\leq n}{}

\displaystyle\cdots\!\!\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\leq j_{k}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\frac{(j_{t}/n)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}}{m_{t}!}(\widetilde{a}_{t}\log(n/j_{t}))^{m_{t}}

\displaystyle-\mathcal{O}\Big(\Big(n-\Big\lceil n^{\varepsilon}-\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\Big\rceil+1\Big)^{k-1}\Big)\prod_{t=1}^{k}\mathrm{e}^{-m_{t}}\frac{m_{t}^{m_{t}}}{m_{t}!}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\prod_{t=1}^{k}\bigg(\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\leq j\leq n}\frac{(j/n)^{\widetilde{a}_{t}}}{m_{t}!}(\widetilde{a}_{t}\log(n/j))^{m_{t}}\bigg)-\mathcal{O}(n^{k-1}),

where the last line follows from the fact that
\mathrm{e}^{-x}x^{x}/\Gamma(x+1)\leq 1
for all
x>0
. We now identify the argument of the sum as
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(P(\widetilde{a}_{t},j)=m_{t}\right)
, where
P(\widetilde{a}_{t})\sim\text{Poi}(\widetilde{a}_{t}\log(n/j))
. Again using the Poisson-gamma duality, we can rewrite this sum as

(5.123)

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\leq j\leq n}{}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}\leq\log(n/j),Y_{t}+V>\log(n/j)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\leq j\leq n}\big(\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}\leq\log(n/j)\right)-\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y_{t}+V\leq\log(n/j)\right)\big),

where
Y_{t}\sim\text{Gamma}(m_{t},\widetilde{a}_{t})
and
V_{t}\sim\text{Exp}(\widetilde{a}_{t})
and both random variables are independent, conditionally on
W_{t}
. With a similar approach as in (
5.86
) and (
5.89
), we can bound this sum from below by

(5.124)

n\mathbb{E}_{W}[\mathrm{e}^{-Y_{t}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{t}\leq\log(n/f(n))\}}]-n\mathbb{E}_{W}[\mathrm{e}^{-(Y_{t}+V_{t})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{t}+V_{t}\leq\log(n/\widetilde{f}(n))\}}]+\mathcal{O}(n^{\varepsilon}),

with
f(n):=\lceil n^{\varepsilon}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\rceil
,
\widetilde{f}(n):=f(n)-1
. As
f(n)\sim\widetilde{f}(n)\sim n^{\varepsilon}
, the desired lower bound follows in an analogous way to the approach used in the upper bound in (
5.90
) through the paragraph following (
5.92
).

In case

(
2
)

, we can as in the upper bound assume without loss of generality that the condition on
c_{t}
holds for all
t\in[k]
. Then, the largest values of
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(P(\widetilde{a}_{t},j)=m_{t}\right)
are attained for
j_{t}
as small as possible. So, by omitting these small
j_{t}
, we obtain the lower bound

(5.125)

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\leq j_{1}\leq n}{}

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+1+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\leq j_{1}\leq n}\!\!\cdots\!\!\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+(k-1)+\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}\leq j_{k}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(P(\widetilde{a}_{t},j_{t})=m_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=1}^{k}\bigg(\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}+(t-1)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}m_{\ell}\leq j\leq n}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(P(\widetilde{a}_{t},j)=m_{t}\right)\bigg).

As in case

(
1
)

, we can write each sum as a difference of expected values. Then, following the same steps as in (
5.93
) through (
5.101
), we obtain the desired matching lower bound in case

(
2
)

for
\varepsilon,\beta>0
sufficiently small. The only difference is that we cannot bound the conditional probability in the second line (
5.94
) from above by one. Instead, the lower bound we obtain is

(5.126)

\displaystyle n\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\bigg[{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+a}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y^{\prime}_{t}\leq\log\Big(n\Big/\Big\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}+(t-1)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}m_{\ell}\Big\rfloor\Big)\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}\geq\log\Big(n\Big/\Big\lceil n^{\varepsilon}+(t-1)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}m_{\ell}\Big\rceil\Big)\right)-n^{-\varepsilon}\frac{a}{1+a}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq n\Bigg[{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+a}\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\Bigg(\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(Y^{\prime}_{t}\leq\log\Big(n\Big/\Big\lfloor n^{\varepsilon}+(t-1)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}m_{\ell}\Big\rfloor\Big)\right)-\mathcal{O}(n^{-\varepsilon})\Bigg)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{O}\bigg(\Big(\frac{a}{1+a}\Big)^{m_{t}}\bigg)\Bigg].

From here on out, the same approach used to bound (
5.96
) from above can be used to yield the lower bound

(5.127)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}\leq n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)\right]\geq\prod_{t=1}^{k}p_{m_{t}}(1-o(n^{-\beta})).

Combined with the upper bound, this yields the desired result and concludes the proof.
∎

We now discuss the necessary changes to the proof for it to hold for the model with
random out-degree
, as introduced in Remark
2.2
(ii)
, as well. The main difference between these two models is the fact that the events
\{n\to i\}
and
\{n\to j\}
are no longer disjoint, where
u\to v
denotes that vertex
u
connects to vertex
v
with an edge directed towards
v
. As a result, the probability, conditional on the vertex-weights, that vertex
n
does
not
connect to vertices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\in[n-1]
now equals

(5.128)

\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\Big(1-\frac{W_{j_{\ell}}}{S_{n-1}}\Big)\qquad\text{instead of}\qquad\Big(1-\frac{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}W_{j_{\ell}}}{S_{n-1}}\Big).

Moreover, as a new vertex can connect to multiple vertices at once, it is no longer necessary to restrict ourselves to indices
j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\ell\in[k]
with
i_{s,\ell}\neq i_{t,j}
for any
\ell,j\in[k],s\in[m_{\ell}],t\in[m_{j}]
. Instead, when
\ell\neq j
,
i_{s,\ell}=i_{t,j}
is allowed for any
s\in[m_{\ell}],t\in[m_{j}]
. That is, the steps at which two vertices increase their degree by one no longer need to be disjoint.

In the proof of the upper bound, this changes the right-hand side of (
5.65
) into

(5.129)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{\ell}<i_{1,\ell}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell},\ell}\leq n,\\
\ell\in[k]\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[

\displaystyle\prod_{t=1}^{k}\prod_{s=1}^{m_{t}}\frac{W_{j_{t}}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{i_{s,t}-1}W_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{u=1}^{k}\!\!\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=j_{u}+1\\
s\neq i_{\ell,t},\ell\in[m_{t}],t\in[k]\end{subarray}}^{j_{u+1}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{u}\bigg(1-\frac{W_{j_{\ell}}}{\sum_{r=1}^{s-1}W_{r}}\bigg)\mathbbm{1}_{E_{n}}\Bigg],

where we change the last term in the expected value due to (
5.128
) and omit the
*
in the inner sum due to the degree increments no longer being disjoint. We can then again use the event
E_{n}^{(1)}
to bound the denominators with deterministic quantities. Then, the exact same steps to obtain the upper bound as in (
5.71
) can be performed. Most importantly, this yields that the difference in the upper bound due to (
5.128
) is no longer present. Furthermore, as we omit the constraint of disjoint indices
i_{s,t}
in (
5.75
), we obtain the exact same upper bound as we would here. As a result, the remaining steps of the proof of the upper bound carry through as well.

The same is true for the lower bound. In fact, in the proof of the lower bound of Lemma
5.10
we argue why we can omit the constraint of disjoint indices
i_{s,t}
in a certain way and still obtain a lower bound in (
5.112
), which is no longer necessary here. Hence, we obtain (
5.115
) without the factor two in front of
\sum_{h=1}^{k}m_{h}
. This change carries through all the steps, so that we arrive at the same desired lower bound. Together with the upper bound, this establishes the desired result.

5.5.
Proof of Lemma
5.11

Proof of Lemma
5.11
.

We aim to bound

(5.130)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon)}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right),

where we recall that
I_{n}(\varepsilon)
from (
5.59
). This is the equivalent of the steps following (
5.51
) in the non-rigorous proof in Section
5.2
. We first assume that
m_{\ell}=c_{\ell}\log n+o(\log n)
for some
c_{\ell}\in[0,1/\log\theta)
for all
\ell\in[k]
. We define

(5.131)

I_{n}(\varepsilon,i):=\{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon):|\{\ell\in[k]:j_{\ell}<n^{\varepsilon}\}|=i\},\qquad i\in[k],

that is,
I_{n}(\varepsilon,i)
denotes the set of indices
\textbf{j}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k})
such that exactly
i
of the indices are smaller than
n^{\varepsilon}
, and note that
I_{n}(\varepsilon)=I_{n}(\varepsilon,1)\cup\cdots\cup I_{n}(\varepsilon,k)
. We then write

(5.132)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\!\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon)}\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\!\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon,i)}\!\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right),

and bound the probability on the right-hand side from above by omitting all events
\{\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\}
whenever
j_{\ell}<n^{\varepsilon}
. This leaves us with

(5.133)

\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}n^{i\varepsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[k]\\
|S|=k-i\end{subarray}}\ \ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\ell}\leq n\\
\ell\in S\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in S\right)+\frac{n^{k\varepsilon}}{(n)_{k}},

where we recall that the
*
on the inner summation denotes that we only consider distinct values of
j_{\ell},\ell\in S
. We isolate the case
i=k
here as in this case no indices are larger than
n^{\varepsilon}
and we hence bound the probability from above by one, whereas
i=k
would yield a contribution of zero in the triple sum. The inner sum can then be dealt with in the same manner as in the derivation of the upper bound in the proof of Lemma
5.10
, to yield an upper bound

(5.134)

\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}n^{-i(1-\varepsilon)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[k]\\
|S|=k-i\end{subarray}}\prod_{\ell\in S}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big(\frac{W}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big)^{m_{\ell}}\right](1+o\big(n^{-\beta}\big)\big)+2n^{-k(1-\varepsilon)},

for some
\beta>0
. It thus remains to show that for any
m_{\ell}=c_{\ell}\log n+o(\log n)
with
c_{\ell}\in[0,1/\log\theta)
we can take
\varepsilon
and a
\beta>0
sufficiently small, such that

(5.135)

n^{-(1-\varepsilon)}=o\Big(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big(\frac{W}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big)^{m_{\ell}}\right]n^{-\beta}\Big).

By Lemma
5.5
, we have for any
\xi>0
and
n
sufficiently large, that

(5.136)

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big(\frac{W}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big)^{m_{\ell}}\right]\geq(\theta+\xi)^{-m_{\ell}}=n^{-c_{\ell}\log(\theta+\xi)+o(1)},

and
n^{-(1-\varepsilon)}=o(n^{-\eta-c_{\ell}\log(\theta+\xi)+o(1)})
when we choose
\eta,\xi,
and
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, since
c_{\ell}\log\theta<1
for any
\ell\in[k]
. As a result,

(5.137)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon)}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)=o\Big(\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big(\frac{W}{\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]+W}\Big)^{m_{\ell}}\right]n^{-\eta}\Big).

We now assume (as before in the proof of Lemma
5.10
without loss of generality) that
m_{\ell}=c_{\ell}\log n+o(\log n)
with
c_{\ell}\in[1/\log\theta,\theta/(\theta-1))
for all
\ell\in[k]
. In this case, the crude bound used above no longer suffices. Now, the aim is to combine the above with a similar approach as at the start of the proof of
[
15
, Theorem
2.9
, Bounded case]
and also use condition

(

C

2
)

in Assumption
2.3
. First, we consider the set of indices
I_{n}(\varepsilon,k)
. To make use of the negative quadrant dependence of the degrees
(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(i))_{i\in[n]}
(see Remark
5.2
and
[
15
, Lemma
7.1
]
), we create an upper bound by considering the event
\{\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\}
. Then, using the tail distribution and the negative quadrant dependency of the degrees under the conditional probability measure
\mathbb{P}_{W}
yields

(5.138)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon,k)}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)\leq\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\neq\ldots\neq j_{k}<n^{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)\right].

We then allow the indices
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}
to take any value between
1
and
n^{\varepsilon}
, to obtain the upper bound

(5.139)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigg(\sum_{i<n^{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(i)\geq m_{\ell}\right)\bigg)\right].

As in the proof of
[
15
, Theorem
2.9
, Bounded case]
, we apply a Chernoff bound to the conditional probability measure
\mathbb{P}_{W}
to obtain

(5.140)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigg(\sum_{i<n^{\varepsilon}}\!\!\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(i)\geq m_{\ell}\right)\bigg)\Bigg]\leq\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigg(\sum_{i<n^{\varepsilon}}\!\!\exp(m_{\ell}(1-u_{i,\ell}+\log u_{i,\ell}))\bigg)\Bigg],

where

(5.141)

u_{i,\ell}:=\frac{1}{m_{\ell}}\sum_{j=i}^{n-1}\frac{W_{i}}{S_{j}}.

This is the equivalent of (
5.52
) in the non-rigorous proof in Section
5.2
. We then introduce the constants
\delta\in(0,1/2),C>kc_{\theta}^{-1}\theta\log(\theta)/(\theta-1)
, and
\alpha>0
(with
c_{\theta}:=1/(2\theta^{2})
). Also, we define the sequences
\zeta^{\prime}_{n}:=(C\log n)^{-\delta/(1-2\delta)}/\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]
,
f(n):=\lceil\log n/\log\log\log n\rceil
, and
g(n):=\lceil\log\log n\rceil
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We then define the events

(5.142)

\displaystyle E^{(3)}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{j}W_{\ell}\geq j\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta^{\prime}_{n}),\text{ for all }(C\log n)^{1/(1-2\delta)}\leq j\leq n\bigg\},

\displaystyle E^{(4)}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\{S_{\lceil\alpha\log n\rceil}\geq c\log n\},

\displaystyle E^{(5)}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\{S_{f(n)}\geq g(n)\}.

The event
E^{(3)}_{n}
is similar to the event
E^{(1)}_{n}
introduced in (
5.63
), but considers a larger range of indices
j
. The particular choice of the lower bound on the indices
j
follows from the fact that we want as much control over the partial sums of the vertex-weights as possible, but need to ensure that
\mathbb{P}((E^{(3)}_{n})^{c})
decays sufficiently fast. It follows that for all three events, the probability of their complement is
o(n^{-\gamma})
for any
\gamma>0
(in the case of
E^{(3)}_{n}
and
E^{(4)}_{n}
when we choose
C
and
\alpha
sufficiently large, respectively) by Lemma
A.2
in the
Appendix
.

We can use these events in the expected value to arrive at the upper bound

(5.143)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigg(\sum_{i<n^{\varepsilon}}\exp(m_{\ell}(1-u_{i,\ell}+\log u_{i,\ell}))\bigg)\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(3)}_{n}\cap E^{(4)}_{n}\cap E^{(5)}_{n}}\right]+\mathbb{P}((E^{(3)}_{n}\cap E^{(4)}_{n}\cap E^{(5)}_{n})^{c}).

By the proof of Corollary
A.3
in the
Appendix
and since
m_{\ell}=c_{\ell}\log n+o(\log n)
with
c_{\ell}>0
, we can bound
u_{i,\ell}
from above uniformly in
i\in[n^{\varepsilon}]
and for any
\ell\in[k]
by
(1+o(1))/(c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right])
. Recalling that
\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]=\theta-1
and that
c_{\ell}\geq 1/\log\theta>1/(\theta-1)
(as
\theta>1
), we find that
u_{i,\ell}<1
for all
i\in[n]
and
\ell\in[k]
as well. Since
x\mapsto 1-x+\log x
is increasing on
(0,1)
we can thus use this upper bound in the first term of (
5.143
) to bound it from above by

(5.144)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}{}

\displaystyle\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigg(\sum_{i<n^{\varepsilon}}\exp\Big(c_{\ell}\log n\Big(1-\frac{1}{c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}+\log\Big(\frac{1}{c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\Big)\Big)(1+o(1))\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(\log n(1+o(1))\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\Big(-(1-\varepsilon)+c_{\ell}\Big(1-\frac{1}{c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}+\log\Big(\frac{1}{c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\Big)\Big)\Big)\Big),

which is the equivalent of (
5.54
) in the non-rigorous proof in Section
5.2
. We then require that

(5.145)

\exp\Big(\log n(1+o(1))\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\Big(\!-(1-\varepsilon)+c_{\ell}\Big(1-\frac{1}{c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}+\log\Big(\frac{1}{c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\Big)\Big)\Big)\Big)=o\Big(\frac{1}{n^{\beta}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{m_{\ell}}\Big),

for some
\beta>0
. As, by Lemma
5.5
,
p_{m_{\ell}}\geq(\theta+\xi)^{-m_{\ell}}=\exp(-\log n(1+o(1))c_{\ell}\log(\theta+\xi))
for any
\xi>0
and
n
sufficiently large, it suffices to show that

(5.146)

\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\Big(-(1-\varepsilon)+c_{\ell}\Big(1-\frac{1}{c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}+\log\Big(\frac{1}{c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\Big)\Big)\Big)<-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}c_{\ell}\log(\theta+\xi)-\beta,

when
\xi,\beta,
and
\varepsilon
are sufficiently small. We show that this strict inequality can be achieved for each term individually, by arguing that we can choose
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
small enough such that

(5.147)

(1-\varepsilon)>c_{\ell}\Big(1-\frac{1}{c_{\ell}(\theta-1)}+\log\Big(\frac{\theta+\xi}{c_{\ell}(\theta-1)}\Big)\Big),\qquad\ell\in[k],

where we note that we have written
\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]
as
\theta-1
. The right-hand side is increasing in
c_{\ell}
when
c_{\ell}\in[1/\log\theta,\theta/(\theta-1))
, so that all
k
inequalities are satisfied when we solve

(5.148)

(1-\varepsilon)>\widetilde{c}\Big(1-\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}+\log\Big(\frac{\theta+\xi}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}\Big)\Big),

with
\widetilde{c}:=\max_{\ell\in[k]}c_{\ell}
. We now show that the right-hand side is strictly smaller than one when
\xi
is sufficiently small. We write

(5.149)

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}\Big(1-\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}+\log\Big(\frac{\theta+\xi}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}\Big(1-\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}+\log\Big(\frac{\theta}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}\Big)\Big)+\widetilde{c}\log\Big(1+\frac{\xi}{\theta}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{c}\Big(1-\frac{1}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}+\log\Big(\frac{\theta}{\widetilde{c}(\theta-1)}\Big)\Big)+\frac{\xi}{\theta-1},

where the final upper bound follows from the fact that
\log(1+x)\leq x
for
x>-1
and
\widetilde{c}<\theta/(\theta-1)
. We denote the first term on the right-hand side by
\kappa=\kappa(\widetilde{c},\theta)
. As
\kappa
is increasing in
\widetilde{c}
when
\widetilde{c}<\theta/(\theta-1)
(which is the case when
c_{\ell}<\theta/(\theta-1)
for all
\ell\in[k]
), we have
\kappa<1
, as
\widetilde{c}<\theta/(\theta-1)
. Thus, setting
\xi<(1-\kappa)(\theta-1)/2
we achieve the desired result. Now, taking
\varepsilon\in(0,1-(\kappa+\xi/(\theta-1)))
, we arrive at (
5.145
) for some small
\eta>0
. Recalling that we can bound the second term in (
5.143
) by
o(n^{-\gamma})
for any
\gamma>0
and that
p_{m_{\ell}}\geq n^{-\nu}
for some
\nu>0
uniformly in
m_{\ell}<c\log n
, we obtain

(5.150)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon,k)}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right)=o\Big(n^{-\eta}\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{m_{\ell}}\Big),

for some small
\eta>0
.

We now consider the remaining sets
I_{n}(\varepsilon,1),\ldots,I_{n}(\varepsilon,k-1)
and aim to bound

(5.151)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon,i)}\mathbb{P}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\text{ for all }\ell\in[k]\right).

Again, using the negative quadrant dependence and introducing the events
E^{(1)}_{n},E^{(3)}_{n},E^{(4)}_{n}
, and
E^{(5)}_{n}
yields the upper bound

(5.152)

\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon,i)}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(1)}_{n}\cap E^{(3)}_{n}\cap E^{(4)}_{n}\cap E^{(5)}_{n}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)\right]+\mathbb{P}\!\left((E^{(1)}_{n}\cap E^{(3)}_{n}\cap E^{(4)}_{n}\cap E^{(5)}_{n})^{c}\right).

The aim is to treat the probabilities of indices which are at most
n^{\varepsilon}
in the same way as when dealing with the indices in
I_{n}(\varepsilon,k)
to reach a bound as in (
5.145
), for which we use the events
E^{(i)}_{n},i=3,4,5
. For the indices which are larger than
n^{\varepsilon}
such an upper bound will not suffice. Instead, we aim to bound
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)
when
n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\ell}\leq n
in a similar way as we bounded
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\right)
from above in the proof of Lemma
5.10
, for which we use the event
E^{(1)}_{n}
.

First, we split the summation over
I_{n}(\varepsilon,i)
over all possible configurations of indices with are at most and at least
n^{\varepsilon}
, similar to (
5.133
). That is,

(5.153)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}{}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\sum_{\textbf{j}\in I_{n}(\varepsilon,i)}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(1)}_{n}\cap E^{(3)}_{n}\cap E^{(4)}_{n}\cap E^{(5)}_{n}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[k]\\
|S|=i\end{subarray}}\ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j_{\ell}<n^{\varepsilon}\\
\ell\in S\end{subarray}}\ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\ell}\leq n\\
\ell\in[k]\backslash S\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\!\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(1)}_{n}\cap E^{(3)}_{n}\cap E^{(4)}_{n}\cap E^{(5)}_{n}}\!\!\prod_{\ell\in S}\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)\!\!\!\!\prod_{\ell\in[k]\backslash S}\!\!\!\!\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)\Bigg].

Using the events
E^{(i)}_{n},i=3,4,5
, we can follow similar steps as above to bound the sum over the indices
j_{\ell}
and the product of probabilities
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)
for
\ell\in S
from above by the deterministic upper bound

(5.154)

\exp\Big(\log n(1+o(1))\sum_{\ell\in S}\Big(-(1-\varepsilon)+c_{\ell}\Big(1-\frac{1}{c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}+\log\Big(\frac{1}{c_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]}\Big)\Big)\Big)\Big)=:n^{C(S)(1+o(1))},

which yields

(5.155)

\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac{n^{i}}{(n)_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[k]\\
|S|=i\end{subarray}}n^{C(S)(1+o(1))}\ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\ell}\leq n\\
\ell\in[k]\backslash S\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{E^{(1)}_{n}}\prod_{\ell\in[k]\backslash S}\!\!\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)\right].

We now proceed to bound each individual probability
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)
when
\ell\in[k]\backslash S
. This follows a similar approach to the upper bound of
\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\right)
in the proof of Lemma
5.10
(as well as the non-rigorous proof provided in Section
5.2
), with a couple of modifications. Introducing indices
j_{\ell}<i_{1}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell}}\leq n
, which denote the steps at which vertex
j_{\ell}
increases its degree, we can write

(5.156)

\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right)=\sum_{j_{\ell}<i_{1}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell}}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{m_{\ell}}\frac{W_{j_{\ell}}}{\sum_{r=1}^{i_{t}-1}W_{r}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}s=j_{\ell}+1\\
s\neq i_{t},t\in[m_{\ell}]\end{subarray}}^{i_{m_{\ell}}-1}\Big(1-\frac{W_{j_{\ell}}}{\sum_{r=1}^{s-1}W_{r}}\Big).

The second product, in comparison to dealing with the event
\{\mathcal{Z}_{n}(j_{\ell})=m_{\ell}\}
, goes up to
i_{m_{\ell}}-1
instead of
n
. This is due to the fact that we now only need to control the connections vertex
j
does and does not make up to its
m_{\ell}^{\text{th}}
connection. Using the same idea as in (
5.66
) and using the event
E^{(1)}_{n}
, we obtain the upper bound

(5.157)

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{\ell}<i_{1}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell}}\leq n}{}

\displaystyle\prod_{t=1}^{m_{\ell}}\frac{W_{j_{\ell}}}{(i_{t}-1)\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-\zeta_{n})-1}\prod_{s=j_{\ell}+1}^{i_{m_{\ell}}-1}\Big(1-\frac{W_{j_{\ell}}}{s\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{\ell}<i_{1}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell}}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{m_{\ell}}\frac{W_{j_{\ell}}}{i_{t}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-2\zeta_{n})}\prod_{s=j_{\ell}+1}^{i_{m_{\ell}}-1}\Big(1-\frac{W_{j_{\ell}}}{s\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})}\Big).

The last step follows from the fact that
(i_{t}-1)(1-\zeta_{n})\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]-1\geq i_{t}(1-2\zeta_{n})\mathbb{E}\left[W\right]
for
n
sufficiently large. Using this in the expected value of (
5.155
) yields

(5.158)

\ \sideset{}{{}^{*}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\ell}\leq n\\
\ell\in[k]\backslash S\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{\ell\in[k]\backslash S}\bigg(\sum_{j_{\ell}<i_{1}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell}}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{m_{\ell}}\frac{W_{j_{\ell}}}{i_{t}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-2\zeta_{n})}\prod_{s=j_{\ell}+1}^{i_{m_{\ell}}-1}\Big(1-\frac{W_{j_{\ell}}}{s\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})}\Big)\bigg)\right].

We can now relabel the vertex-weights
W_{j_{\ell}}
as
W_{\ell}
,
\ell\in[k]\backslash S
. This does not change the expected value and is possible since the indices
j_{\ell}
are distinct for all
\ell\in[k]\backslash S
. Directly after this, we omit the requirement that the indices
j_{\ell}
are distinct, which is now of no consequence as the weights have been relabelled already. We hence arrive at the upper bound

(5.159)

\prod_{\ell\in[k]\backslash S}\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\ell}\leq n}\ \sum_{j_{\ell}<i_{1}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell}}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{m_{\ell}}\frac{W_{\ell}}{i_{t}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-2\zeta_{n})}\!\prod_{s=j_{\ell}+1}^{i_{m_{\ell}}-1}\!\!\Big(1-\frac{W_{\ell}}{s\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n})}\Big)\!\Bigg],

where the product can be taken out of the expected value due to the independence of the vertex-weights
W_{1},\ldots W_{\ell}
. As a result, we can deal with each of expected values individually. Following the same approach as in (
5.71
) and setting
a_{\ell}:=W_{\ell}/(\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1+\zeta_{n}))
, we obtain the upper bound

(5.160)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[{}

\displaystyle\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\ell}\leq n}\ \sum_{j_{\ell}<i_{1}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell}}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{m_{\ell}}\frac{W_{\ell}}{i_{t}\mathbb{E}\left[W\right](1-2\zeta_{n})}\Big(\frac{i_{m_{\ell}}}{j_{\ell}}\Big)^{-a_{\ell}}\Bigg]\Big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[a_{\ell}^{m_{\ell}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{n^{\varepsilon}\leq j_{\ell}\leq n}\ \sum_{j_{\ell}<i_{1}<\ldots<i_{m_{\ell}}\leq n}\prod_{t=1}^{m_{\ell}}i_{t}^{-1}\Big(\frac{i_{m_{\ell}}}{j_{\ell}}\Big)^{-a_{\ell}}\Bigg]\Big(\frac{1+\zeta_{n}}{1-2\zeta_{n}}\Big)^{m_{\ell}}\Big(1+\mathcal{O}\big(n^{-\varepsilon}\big)\Big).

Let us first take out the term
(a_{\ell}j_{\ell})^{a_{\ell}}
from the inner sums. We then observe that the summand of the inner sum over the indices
i_{1},\ldots,i_{m_{\ell}}
is decreasing, so that we can bound it from above almost surely by the multiple integrals

(5.161)

\int_{j_{\ell}}^{n}\int_{x_{1}}^{n}\cdots\int_{x_{m_{\ell}-1}}^{n}\prod_{t=1}^{m_{\ell}-1}x_{t}^{-1}x_{m_{\ell}}^{-(1+a_{\ell})}\,\mathrm{d}x_{m_{\ell}}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{1}=a_{\ell}^{-m_{\ell}}j_{\ell}^{-a_{\ell}}\bigg(1-\Big(\frac{n}{j_{\ell}}\Big)^{-a_{\ell}}\!\sum_{s=0}^{m_{\ell}-1}a_{\ell}^{s}\frac{(\log(n/j_{\ell}))^{s}}{s!}\bigg).

The equality follows from (
A.23
) in Lemma
A.4
with
a=j_{\ell},b=n,c=a_{\ell}
, and
k=m_{\ell}
. Again reintroducing the term
a_{\ell}^{m_{\ell}}j^{a_{\ell}}
in the expected value in (
5.160
), we arrive at

(5.162)

1-\sum_{s=0}^{m_{\ell}-1}(n/j_{\ell})^{-a_{\ell}}\frac{(a_{\ell}\log(n/j_{\ell}))^{s}}{s!}=\mathbb{P}_{W}\!\left(P(a_{\ell})\geq m_{\ell}\right),

where
P(a_{\ell})\sim\text{Poi}(a_{\ell}\log(n/j_{\ell}))
, conditionally on
W_{\ell}
